WYOMiNG SALES/USE TAX REPORTING IM?ORMATION
1.
Your filing frequency is Monthly beginning: 12/1/2020. Quarterly filers will be setup on calendar quarter.
If you are a quarterly filer, your first return may be for a portion of a calendar quarter.
2.
You will receive your return approximately the first week of the month in which it is due. Example: MONTHLY filers;
January return will be received first half of February and it must be post marked on or before the last day of February.
QUARTERLY filers; January, February, and March returns will he received first half of April and must be post marked
on or before the last day of April.
3.
Failure to receive a return from the Department of Revenue does not relieve you from the responsibility of
filing and paying the tax due on or before the due date. Returns must be filed even if no sales were made or
any tax due.
4.
The postmark date determines the timeliness of your return. Returns with a late postmark are subject to penalty and
interest.
5.
Please notify the Excise Tax Division at the Cheyenne Office in writing if there is a change of address or ownership.
Be sure to include your Wyoming Sales/Use tax license number on any correspondence and/or remittance sent to the
Department to ensure timely processing.

Mayor Patrick Collins and members of City Council,
I hope this letter finds you well during the hard times that we are all currently facing. I am
writing to you today to introduce ourselves as a new small business in the city of Cheyenne,
WY. We are Tres Amigos Incorporated, doing business as Tres Amigos Family Mexican
Restaurant. We have a proposed business site of 2414 W Lincolnway, Cheyenne WY 82001.
There are three partners in this business, Gustavo Camorlinga, Ahias Ambrosio, Jonathan
lniguez. We have all been friends for many years, and together we have 33 years of combined
experience in the restaurant business. After a lot of thought, we have decided to go into
business together and truly believe that your community is the perfect place to begin our
journey.
We have built a full menu with delicious options for everyone that include small platters up to
large combinations and unique house specialties, not to mention the amazing cocktails and
margaritas that we have worked hard to perfect that the community will enjoy while
experiencing a relaxed environment and great customer service, we can accommodate
customer in our larger dining area and larger parties or work events in a more secluded part of
the building.
I appreciate this opportunity to introduce our company and services to you and express my
eagerness to get started and bring a new Mexican Restaurant to you your city. If you have any
questions or concerns, please contact me at any time.
Respecfully,
fl
4jnathan lniguez
Tres Amigos Incorporated
970.631.9875
Jiniguez15@gmail.com

Background:  
The item for consideration is an Annexation.  This is approved by Ordinance.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
 
Introduction and Referral

  Planning and Development Department

ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE ANNEXING TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, LAND LOCATED NORTHWEST OF AND ADJACENT TO 
THE INTERSECTION OF DELL RANGE BOULEVARD AND WHITNEY 
ROAD.” 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.
That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows: 
(a) 
That Gysel Whitney, LLC, owners of:
A parcel of un-platted land being situated in a portion of the southeast one-quarter 
of Section 23, Township 14 North, Range 66 West, of the 6th Principal 
Meridian; being more particularly described as follows:
Considering the east line of said southeast quarter of Section 23 being monumented 
by a found iron rod at the east one quarter corner and a found aluminum cap at the 
southeast corner of Section 23. said line bears S00°26'28”W at a distance of 
2,644.52 feet with all bearings hereon relative thereto.  
Beginning at the intersection of the south right of way of Dell Range Boulevard 
and the east right of way Whitney Road; thence along said south right of way the 
following courses and distances: S89°33'10"W a distance of 2707.51 feet to a point 
of change in right of way width; thence N00°40'06"E a distance of 10.00 feet to a 
point; thence N89°16'58"W a distance of 200.08 feet to a point; thence leaving said 
south right of way N00°11'03"W a distance of 80.00 feet to a point on the north 
right of way of said Dell Range Boulevard; thence along said north right of way the 
following courses and distances: S89°16'58"E a distance of 200.05 feet to a point; 
thence N89°33'10"E a distance of 527.20 feet to a point; thence leaving said north 
right of way line N00°28'26"E a distance of 1531.40 feet to a point; thence 
S89°33'14"E a distance of 2180.40 feet to a point on said east right of way of 
Whitney Road; thence along said east right of way S00°26'28"W a distance of 
1587.42 feet to the point of beginning. 
 
have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real 
property described herein and to make it a part thereof; and  
(b) 
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as Exhibit 
A, said map prepared by Adam E. Deschler, a Professional Land Surveyor, licensed
in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 12045; and 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; and 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; and 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and other 
services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, be 
furnished; and
(f)
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned A-2
Agricultural by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and its
governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the entire annexed 
area. 

Section 2. 
That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3. 
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to the 
official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
Section 4. 
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared illegal 
or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5. 
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE GOVERNING BODY 
April 12, 2021 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
UDC-21-00079:  Annexation of Gysel Whitney LLC (Whitney Ranch, 4th Filing)  
REQUEST: 
An annexation of 81.8 acres of land  
LOCATION: 
Northwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and 
Whitney Road.  
APPLICANT: 
Whitney Ranch, LLC 
AGENT: 
Brad Emmons, AVI, PC  
OWNER: 
Gysel Whitney, LLC 
PURPOSE: 
To allow grading under City jurisdiction for future urban-level development 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Planner II  Development 
 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-21-00079
April 12, 2021
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
East Dell Range Blvd / US 30 Corridor Study 
 
Whitney Road Corridor Plan 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing of 81.8 acres of land for future residential and commercial 
development. The County Assessor presently shows the tract of land as part of one larger unplatted parcel. 
The land is currently undeveloped in the County. A zone change is proposed concurrently with this 
annexation. Both applications are anticipated to come to final consideration before the Governing Body at 
the same meeting.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by a submission 
to change the zoning from County A-2 Agricultural to the City zone of AG Agricultural. While the AG zone 
does not allow urban-style uses, it is appropriate as a holding zone until development plans are finalized. The 
jurisdiction prior to finalizing the development patterns. A preliminary plat for the area, with lots and blocks 
designed for residential and commercial development, was approved in August 2020. It is anticipated that a 
final plat and a rezoning application would come before the Governing Body within a year of this annexation. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned A-2 Agricultural in the County. The property is concurrently proposed to 
be annexed and rezoned to the AG Agricultural. The AG zone is generally described as an agricultural or 
natural area zone, where most typical urban-style uses are prohibited.  The AG zone is applicable to 
areas where significant natural lands or open spaces are to be protected, or as an interim or holding 
zone for areas that may be further developed in the long term, but where minimal development activity 
in the interim is acceptable and will not prematurely establish development patterns.

UDC-21-00079
April 12, 2021
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the occupants of 
future development once it is built. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to 
City Fire and Police and sanitation services. The property will also be connected to City water 
and sanitary sewer instead of having wells and septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The contiguity to the west and the proximity to urban-style development to the 
south makes the parcel a natural geographic part of the City. The development of this property 
will benefit those who live within the City and will be a part of the social and economic fabric 
that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. City water and sewer lines exist in the vicinity and could be 
extended with the development of the property. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation are about 8.2% contiguous, along the western edge.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
 

UDC-21-00079
April 12, 2021
5)
If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on March 12, 2021. The 
public hearing for this annexation is scheduled for April 12, 2021. Twenty (20) business days 
prior to the announced public hearing is March 15, 2021. The earliest confirmation of delivery 
was dated March 13, 2021. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
East Dell Range Blvd / US 30 Corridor Study 
 
Whitney Road Corridor Plan 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with an urban residential style of development. These areas are expected to 
develop with full access to urban services  such areas are best served by being within City limits. 
Policies in PlanCheyenne support development within the Urban Service Boundary and the utilization of 
existing infrastructure.  
 
The East Dell Range Blvd / US 30 Corridor Study and the Whitney Road Corridor Plan  both anticipate 
that this area to develop with urban-style commercial and residential uses. Urban-style uses are best 
served by being within City limits and connecting to City services. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, the following comments were received: 
- 
Annexation is appropriate from an environmental health perspective 
- 
Annexation will bring Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road and the intersection into city 
limits 
 
 
 
 
 
 

UDC-21-00079
April 12, 2021
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
services typically available to urban areas are available to this property.   
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property.  
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Aerial, Current City Limits, and Future Land Use Map 
2. Submitted Application 
3. Applicant-submitted annexation map 
4. Supporting photos

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________ 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM 
COUNTY A-2 AGRICULTURAL TO AG AGRICULTURAL FOR LAND 
LOCATED NORTHWEST OF AND ADJACENT TO THE INTERSECTION OF 
DELL RANGE BOULEVARD AND WHITNEY ROAD.” 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment,
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
Section 2. That on April 5, 2021, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended 
approval of said zone change. 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as: 
A parcel of land located in the southeast one-quarter of Section 23, Township 14 North 
Range 66 West of the 6th Principal Meridian. 
Considering the east line of said southeast quarter of Section 23 being monumented by a 
found iron rod at the east one quarter corner and a found aluminum cap at the southeast 
corner of Section 23. Said line bears S00°26'28”W at a distance of 2,644.52 feet with all 
bearings hereon relative thereto.  
Commencing at the east quarter corner of said Section 23, thence S00°26'28”W at a 
distance of 1,106.49 feet along the east line of the southeast quarter; thence leaving said 
east line of said southeast quarter N89°33'14”W at a distance of 40.00 feet to a point on 
the westerly right of way line of Whitney Road and the true point of beginning. Thence 
continuing along said westerly right of way line S00°26'28”W at a distance of 1498.65 feet 
to a point on the northerly right of way line of Dell Range Boulevard; thence along said 
northerly right of way S89°33'10”W at a distance of 2100.65 feet; thence leaving said 
northerly right of way N00°26'28”E at a distance of 1531.40 feet; thence S89°33'14”E at a 
distance of 2100.40 feet to the true point of beginning. 
is hereby changed to AG Agricultural. 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication, 
and upon the official filing of the affiliated Gysel Whitney, LLC Annexation Map with the County Clerk 
and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming.

ORDINANCE NO. _________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE RESCINDING FOOTNOTE 1 IN ORDINANCE NO. 
4314 APPROPRIATING MONEYS FOR THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING FOR THE VARIOUS PURPOSES IN CONDUCTING THE 
MUNICIPAL GOVERNMENT OF SAID CITY AND FIXING THE 
AMOUNT OF GENERAL AND SPECIAL TAXES AS PART OF THE 
REVENUE REQUIRED TO MEET THE SAID APPROPRIATION, ALL 
FOR THE FISCAL YEAR BEGINNING JULY 1, 2020, AND ENDING 
JUNE 30, 2021.” 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the following Footnote that currently exists in Ordinance No. 4314, is 
rescinded: 
 
FOOTNOTE 1: Authorized General Fund full time positions for FY2021 shall not 
exceed 382. 
 
 
Section 2.  That all other sections currently existing in Ordinance No. 4314 not set out 
within this ordinance remain unchanged. 
 
 
Section 3.  That this ordinance shall become effective upon its passage and publication.

expenditures: Emergency Microgrants - $51,328.26.
FOOTNOTE 9: Consistent with Senate Enrolled Act 1 (SEA 1) of the Wyoming Legislature’s 2020 Special

Footnote 11.

Consistent with Senate Enrolled Act 1 (SEA 1) of the Wyoming Legislature’s 2020 Special
Session, Section 1(b)(iv), any CARES Act dollars received by the City of Cheyenne after reimbursement to
the City for direct COVID-19 related expenditures through SEA 1 or otherwise, not spent as set forth in
footnotes 10, 11, 12 shall next be appropriated to eligible expenditures budgeted to be funded by reserves
in FY2021. Said expenditures are related to the $775,000 designated for the Civic Center and the $260,000
Minimum Revenue Guarantee for the Airport.
FOOTNOTE 12: There are General Fund line items historically rolled over each fiscal year that will not be
rolled over in FY 2021, either in part or in total, and will instead be used to fund $17,500 for Safe Harbor,
$15,000 for the Cheyenne Animal Shelter for shelter services, $25,000 for Laramie County Senior Services,
and $58,500 for the Cheyenne Animal Shelter for animal control services. An additional $57,687 will be
used to fund the Fire Department. These rollover line items total $132,685 and include $50,000 in Belvoir
Ranch Planning/Development (001-21-2113-40-43906); $13,216 in Right of Way Purchases/Professional

and $13,273 in Capital Improvement Expenses (001-21-2113-40-32107).
The remaining funds in the
amount of $35,602 will be transferred from the Miscellaneous Department insurance line item as a result
of a lower property, liability and crime coverage rate increase than what was projected.
FOOTNOTE 13: Move $61,998 from the Miscellaneous Department insurance line item to the Community
Recreation and Events Aquatics Division as a result of a lower property, liability and crime coverage
insurance rate increase than was projected.

Note 1: The freon removal fee must be paid in addition to disposal fee for all refrigerators, freezers, and 
air conditioners unless freon removal has been certified by a licensed repairman.  
 
Note 2: A “building,” for the purposes of Section 8.44.010 D of City Code, is defined as a non-residential 
premises using a water meter.  Buildings will be assessed at least a “Basic Rate Fee” for each collection 
point (i.e., container or set of containers) or charges based on the type and quantity of containers and 
frequency of pickups as outlined in this Solid Waste Fee Schedule.  Multiple collocated, non-residential 
buildings owned by a single property owner will be assessed at least a “Basic Rate Fee” equal to the 
number of collection points.  If 1.5 Cubic Yard or larger city dumpsters are used, the Basic Rate Fee is 
included in the fees for those containers.  Charges are normally included in water and sewer bills issued 
to property owners or agents of such premises by the Board of Public Utilities. 
  
Note 3: If material is left outside of containers (dumpsters) requiring manual pickup, the owner of the 
property may be assessed an additional cleanup fee for manpower and equipment time.  
 
Note 4: City residents must show identification (driver’s license, etc.) and certify that material is their 
personal household waste to receive reduced Transfer Station rates.   
 
Note 5: Electronic Components: Charges to commercial entities for recycling of electronic components 
are based on the City’s cost for recycling plus 10% for handling and processing. Contact the Solid Waste 
Division for current prices.  Electronic components (computers, televisions, cell phones, etc.) contain 
hazardous materials and must not be disposed of in the Landfill. Recycling of these components is free 
to city/county residents at the Transfer Station. 
 
Note 6: Tires larger than 20” will be charged by weight. 
 
Note 7: Residents must present documentation from a doctor to qualify for “Special Attention” services.  
Documentation must be updated on an annual basis to continue in the program. 
 
Note 8: To qualify for the reduced rate 60 Gallon Recycle Residential Container, residents must also 
have a 60 Gallon Automated Trash Container. Residents may choose not to accept a recycle container; 
however, there will be no reduction in the residential automated collection fee. 
 
Note 9: Overweight or over-packed Roll-Off Containers will be charged double the disposal fee.  
Overloaded cardboard containers will be charged based on time required to empty the container. 
 
Note 10: Residential trash and recycle service may be suspended for full months only. Full month 
suspension dates are based on billing cycles as defined by the Board of Public Utilities. 
 
Note 11: The optional yard waste collection program may not be suspended, only discontinued. 
Residents who pay for the optional yard waste container receive pickup of up to one cubic yard of limbs, 
tree trimmings, hedge clippings and similar material per pickup.  Residents must cut items to less than 
four-foot lengths and contact the Sanitation Division to schedule pickup.  
 
Note 12: Place automated trash, recycle and yard waste containers a minimum of two feet from any 
object, i.e., vehicles, trailers, fences, poles, trees, other containers, etc., and be sure the area in front of 
the container (toward the street) is clear of obstructions. A Container Placement Fee is charged each 
time a driver must exit the vehicle to move a container so it may be safely dumped without striking a 
nearby object. The fee will not apply if a container is blown over by the wind or other act of nature.   
 
Note 13:  These charges apply to customers who do not get a water bill.  Billing must be done manually 
by Solid Waste Staff.     
 
Note 14:  The Landfill Manager may request to inspect the origin of the soil and require testing to verify 
the soil is uncontaminated.  The customer will pay for any testing. 
 
Note 15:  The Public Works Director or Deputy Director may negotiate costs and terms for unusual 
requests to dispose of material at the Landfill.  Examples include but are not limited to:  material from 
large commercial demolition or remodeling projects; material such as dirt that may be needed by the 
City; and material from accidents or natural disasters.  Testing for hazardous materials may be required 
in these instances.

ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE 
(UDC) TO INCREASE THE TOTAL LOT COVERAGE OF THE MD1, 
MD2, P-CV1, P-CV2, AND P-CV3 LOT TYPES TO 80%.”  
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2. That the intent of this text amendment is to promote water conservation and 
sustainable development. 
 
Section 3. That on April 19, 2021 the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
 
Section 4. That Section 5.1.5.d lot type MD1 and lot type MD2 be amended to allow a 
Total Lot Coverage (max.) of 80% and the UDC graphic be modified to reflect the amendment.   
 
Section 5. That Section 5.6.1.d lot type P-CV1, P-CV2 and P-CV3 be amended to allow a 
Total Lot Coverage (max.) of 80% and the UDC graphic be modified to reflect the amendment.   
 
Section 6. The aforementioned Section of the UDC is hereby amended as set out within 
this ordinance and sections currently existing within the UDC not set out within this ordinance 
remain unchanged. 
 
Section 7. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable.  If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 8. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication.

Wyoming Tribune Eagle 
By Alessandra at 8:13 am, Apr 21, 2021

PLN-21-00002  
 
 
April 19, 2021 
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
April 19, 2021 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to increase permitted lot coverage for multi-dwelling and 
civic building lot types. 
 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLN-21-00002 
PREPARED BY: 
Mark Christensen, Planner II, Planning and Development 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
to increase maximum permitted lot coverage to 80% for the MD1, MD2, P-CV1, P-CV2, and P-CV3 lot types 
as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the request meets the review criteria of 
UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of Plan Cheyenne, and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
For each lot type contained within the Unified Development Code there is a total lot coverage determined. 
The maximum total lot coverage is important as it not only determines how much non-permeable surface 
is permitted on any given lot, but also prescribes the amount of landscaping required. The lower the 
percentage specified for total lot coverage, the more landscaping the lot is required to have. Staff has 
determined that more required landscaping has been detrimental to multi-family development within the 
community. Requiring more landscaping and less lot coverage on multi-family lots results in developments 
being less dense, more water intensive, and more expensive. Furthermore, staff has determined that 
requiring more landscaping on civic building lot types expends significant taxpayer dollars.  Staff feels that 
increasing the maximum permitted lot coverage to 80% in the MD1, MD2, P-CV1, P-CV2, and P-CV3 will 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLN-21-00002  
 
 
April 19, 2021 
 
allow developers to provide more units, increase water conservation, and allow for responsible 
stewardship of taxpayer revenue in the P District. 
 
DISCUSSION: 
 
Article 5 in the Unified Development Code outlines the Zoning Regulations for the City of Cheyenne. Each 
Zoning District allocates certain lot types that determine the permitted lot area, lot frontage, setbacks, 
maximum lot coverage, and building height for any given development that occurs in the District. Total 
lot coverage is based on a percentage of the lot area and is defined as:  
 
The portion of the lot covered by the principal building, accessory building, and any structure or 
constructed element which impedes infiltration of stormwater into the ground or disrupts 
vegetated surfaces. For purposes of lot coverage standards, structures or constructed elements 
that allow some infiltration, such as decks, patios and driveways, porous pavers or concrete, or 
other elements that disturb or compact permeable ground cover shall be counted towards the 
total surface coverage. Required formal open spaces such as Plazas, Courtyards or Patios may 
be excluded from the coverage calculation when they are designed to infiltrate all run-off within 
the open space or site. 
 
Essentially, the definition of total lot coverage is the total non-permeable surface on the lot. In addition 
to determining the non-permeable surface, the maximum total lot coverage also dictates the required 
landscaping on site. Landscaping is calculated for both required trees and required ground cover and 
non-tree landscape features. The formula is as follows (where landscape area is the inverse of the total 
lot coverage i.e. a lot coverage of 90% is required 10% landscape area): 
 
Required Landscaping = (Total Lot Area X Landscape Area) / 500 
 
Staff has received feedback from the development community and has determined that the current 
maximum total lot coverage requirements for the MD1 and MD2 are difficult to implement for two 
reasons. First, it limits the buildable area of the development; thus, resulting in fewer units being built. 
This has an adverse effect on the housing supply in the community and contributes to reducing housing 
affordability with reduced supply. In order for developers to maximize development potential and 
deliver more units, they must build higher resulting in higher costs. Secondly, it is difficult for developers 
to meet landscaping requirements. A development under the current limitations of 60% lot coverage in 
the MD1 lot type developing an acre is required to provide between 23 and 34 trees, not including 
street trees. Furthermore, it is required to provide a combination of shrubs (.25 points), ground cover 
(.25-.75 points per 500 square feet), and grasses (1 point) to total 34.8 landscaping points. This amount 
of landscaping is not only expensive, but also requires significant water resources. The same issues 
emerge for landscaping in the P (Public District) for the P-CV1, P-CV2, and P-CV3 lot types; except for in 
these situations it is taxpayer funded agencies utilizing large amounts of water and paying for high 
landscaping costs.  
 
The solution staff has crafted for the situation is increasing the permitted maximum lot coverage to 80% 
in the MD1, MD2, P-CV1, P-CV2, and P-CV3 lot types. In the situation illustrated in the previous 
paragraph, the same development of an acre for the MD1 lot type will be required between 11 to 17 
trees and landscaping points to total 17.4. In addition to the reduced landscaping, the developer will 
also be able to develop more of the site and potentially provide additional units. In the P-CV3 lot type 
with a development of 5 acres, which many school developments fall into, it decreases potential tree 
requirements from a range of 145 to 217 down to 58 to 87. Staff is confident that the amendment will 

PLN-21-00002  
 
 
April 19, 2021 
 
provide the necessary landscaping to ensure the site is not only aesthetically pleasing, but also maintain 
fiscal and environmental responsibility. 
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
 
The proposed amendments include increasing the permitted lot coverage for the MD1, MD2, P-CV1, P-
CV2, and P-CV3 lot types to 80%. These amendments are shown on the following tables: 
 
 
Figure 1: MD1 Lot Type 
 
 
 
Figure 2: MD2 Lot Type 
 
80% 
80% 

PLN-21-00002  
 
 
April 19, 2021 
 
 
Figure 3: P-CV1 Lot Type 
 
 
 
Figure 4: P-CV2 Lot Type 
 
 
 
Figure 5: P-CV3 Lot Type 
 
 
 
 
 
80% 
80% 
80% 

PLN-21-00002  
 
 
April 19, 2021 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or is 
necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
This proposed text amendment is consistent with the principles and policies within PlanCheyenne. 
Specifically, the proposed amendment addresses the following policies and principles: 
 
• 
Policy 2.5.A: Community housing needs met. 
Synopsis: Planning staff has received feedback from the development community that it is 
difficult to develop multi-dwelling housing with landscaping standards and building area 
limitations with current total lot coverage requirements. Increasing the maximum coverage 
to 80% will permit a larger variety of housing to be developed with more sustainable 
landscaping practices. 
 
• 
Policy 5.4.A: Water conservation. 
Synopsis: Reducing the required landscaping required in these areas, notably ground 
coverage, will promote water conservation in the City of Cheyenne. 
 
• 
Foundation 7: Developing in a Fiscally Responsible Way. 
Synopsis: By limiting the required water resources to irrigate landscaping on new public 
facilities in the P-Public District lot types, the City, County, State, and School District will be 
able to develop in a more fiscally responsible way. 
 
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-602 (a):  
The governing body shall specify how regulations, restrictions and the 
district boundaries are to be determined, established, enforced, 
amended, supplemented or otherwise changed. 
Assessment:  
State statute states that the governing body shall specify how regulations 
regarding zoning are established and enforced.  The governing body 
possesses the authority to amend regulations and therefore can amend 
the Unified Development Code to reflect this text amendment. 
 
WYO 15-1-601 (a):  
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
(i) Regulate and restrict the: 
(D) Density of the population 
(E) Location and use of buildings, structures and land for trade, 
industry, residence or other purposes. 
Assessment:  
This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code for 
the purposes of regulating the location of buildings and density of the 
population; thus, fulfilling the requirements of this statute.  
 

PLN-21-00002  
 
 
April 19, 2021 
 
WYO 15-1-601(d):  
All regulations shall be made: 
(ii) With reasonable consideration, among other things, of the character 
of the district and its peculiar suitability for particular uses; 
(iii) With a view to conserving the value of buildings and encouraging the 
most appropriate use of land throughout the city or town. 
Assessment: 
This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code for 
the purposes of encouraging sustainable water practices suitable to the 
climate of the City of Cheyenne.  Regulations of this nature are allowable 
under state statue and may be modified by the Governing Body.    
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment will not adversely impact the general health, safety and public welfare of 
Cheyenne. 
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. Increasing the 
permitted lot coverage in the MD1, MD2, P-CV1, P-CV2, and P-CV3 lot types will promote water 
conservation, foster fiscal responsibility, and encourage more multi-dwelling housing development in the 
community. The amendments accomplish several policies within PlanCheyenne and are enabled by 
Wyoming State Zoning statutes. Lastly, increasing the housing supply while promoting water conservation 
will improve the overall health, safety, and welfare of the community. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code to increase 
permitted lot coverage for multi-dwelling and civic building lot types noting the request meets the 
review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 


--- END OF FILE ---

Section 1. That various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages,
of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code ofthe City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, are
hereby amended as set out in this ordinance.
5.12.020
Definitions.
“Delivery service” means the taking of orders and providing the transporting and delivery
of alcoholic liquor and malt beverage products to patrons at a residence, organization or business
for a fee, in violation of the provisions of the chapter. and a liquor licensee’s process by which
their employee or contractor provides alcohol to a purchaser.
1.
“Delivery” means the act of providing alcohol to a purchaser.
2.
“Delivery Employee” means the licensee’s employee or contractor responsible for
delivering alcohol to the purchaser.
3.
“Sealed” means in its original package and unopened-factory sealed (bottle of wine or
spirits/case of beer), in a plastic bag, heat sealed closed, or in a container that has a
breakable seal incorporated in the top.
4.
“Serve” means the act of delivering alcohol to a purchaser or delivery employee or
contractor on the licensed premises.
5.12.070
Restrictions upon license or permit applicants and holders.
B.
Retail Liquor Licensees.
3.
A licensee shall not may provide any alcoholic liquor or malt beverage delivery
services in conjunction with the licensee’s business operations pursuant to Wyo. Stat. Section 12-
5-601.
a.
Retail liquor licensees, microbrewery permittees, winery permittees,
satellite winery permittees, and satellite manufacturing permittees, may be permitted to
provide delivery service for the delivery of alcohol to customers.
Microbreweries and
wineries shall only be permitted to provide delivery of their manufactured product.
Delivery services shall be subject to the following:
i.
No order shall be received from nor delivery made to a person under twenty-
one (21) years of age.
Each purchaser shall be required to provide a valid
driver’s license or valid government issued identification upon delivery.
ii.
No part of a publicly owned sidewalk, highway, street, or alley shall be used
for taking orders or conducting sales.
No sales shall be made to intoxicated
individuals pursuant to Section 5.12.160(C) of this chapter.
iii. Alcohol shall only be delivered to customers in a sealed package.
iv. Delivery shall not be made to or on public property and shall not be delivered
outside the city of Cheyenne boundaries unless otherwise allowed by Wyoming
Statute, resolution of the Laramie County Commissioners, or order of the
Governor of Wyoming.
1

v.
Delivery service employees shall have completed an alcohol server training
programs as approved by the Wyoming Liquor Division and pursuant to Section
5.12.090 of this chapter; shall be at least twenty-one (21) years of age; shall
maintain a valid driver’s license; shall maintain valid vehicle insurance
coverage as required by Wyoming Statute; shall be an employee or contractor
of the licensee; and shall be required to confirm the identification of the
purchaser.
vi. The licensee and!or the delivery service employee or contractor shall notify the
Cheyenne Police Department of any criminal incident or criminal activity
which is observed or occurs as soon as reasonably possible.
vii. All sales shall occur on the licensee’s premise by in-person purchase, online
order, order by app, or order by phone. Payment shall be received at the time
of purchase. Payment may not occur upon delivery.
viii.
Licensees shall keep a record of each delivery service transaction for a
period of no less than two (2) years which shall be made available to the city
upon request. The record shall include, at minimum, the name and address of
the purchaser, date and time of order, address of delivery, name of delivery
employee or contractor, and the product purchased and delivered.
ix. Delivery shall only be made to the purchaser.
x.
Delivery shall be prohibited for kegs.
xi. Delivery shall be completed the day of the sale.
4.
All retail liquor licensees, microbrewery permittees, winery permittees, satellite
winery permittees, and satellite manufacturing permittees, may establish a delivery service without
submitting an amended operation plan, provided that the licensee submits written notice to the city
clerk of its intent to provide delivery services. Failure to provide notice shall prohibit the licensee
from providing delivery services.
5.
All retail liquor licensees, microbrewery permittees, winery permittees, satellite
winery permittees, and satellite manufacturing permittees, may establish curbside pick-up services
at the licensed premise without submitting an amended operation plan.
6.
Any violation of the aforementioned requirements, restrictions, or procedures by
any licensee, licensee’s employee, or contractor shall be a misdemeanor punishable pursuant to
the general penalty provisions set forth in Chapter 1.24.010 of the city code.
7.
Should any portion of this ordinance be in conflict with any other city of Cheyenne
ordinance, this ordinance is deemed to control or supersede.
5.12.160
Violations.
E. No person may engage in the business of a delivery service involving alcoholic liquor
and malt
